If you are thinking about training fixes, spend extra time on the internal-analysis section because it points directly to the missing capability: conditional trust in retrieved evidence.26:T4fc,BAER enforces candidate symmetry through a signed-score interface: swapping the two responses should flip the sign of the judgment but preserve its magnitude, i.e. s(Tx) = -s(x). Each raw judging protocol is represented as an expert signal and decomposed into a signed preference and a candidate-invariant reliability feature. The expert bank contains nine protocols: bidirectional direct judging, self-consistency, chain-of-thought, rubric-based judging, score-then-choose, PRePair, PriDe, CalibraEval, and an internal deliberate A-versus-B expert. Experts may be run under both display orders, and probabilities are mapped back to the canonical candidate. BAER estimates each expert’s average preference probability and order instability, then builds raw and reliability-shrunk log-odds; unstable experts are downweighted. It defines three heads: evidence stacking, which combines expert signals; reliability-based routing, which selects an expert per pair using candidate-invariant reliability features; and candidate-blind reference verification, which constructs a reference without seeing either candidate and then verifies candidates against it. Development data select one head for each benchmark-backbone condition, and that choice is frozen before test evaluation.27:T426,The paper introduces an MMORPG-style sandbox plus a curated benchmark of collaborative tasks. The simulator supports up to 1,000 concurrent agents and includes more than 380 items, 144 mob types, over 70 NPCs, and 13 high-level API tools for actions such as combat, crafting, gathering, and navigation. The high-level API design is important: it removes much of the low-level control burden so that benchmark scores reflect collaboration rather than 3D manipulation skill. The benchmark contains 100 human-annotated tasks and 100 LLM-augmented variants across combat, crafting, gathering, trading, exploration, survival, construction, and coordination. Tasks require 3 to 20 agents over 25 to 55 rounds, with asymmetric roles and no access to other agents’ internal states. The proposed Causal Collaboration Effectiveness metric builds a causal action graph over trajectories and measures the fraction of actions that contributed to the final outcome, using LLMs mainly to help infer action-level causal links rather than to issue a single subjective judgment.28:T50d,The core contribution is COMPASS, a controlled evaluation framework built from Visual Genome scene graphs. For each image, the authors sample a coherent subgraph via a random walk of up to ten steps, preserving objects, their attributes, and pairwise relations. From this shared primitive set, they generate captions with different structures and complexity levels. This enables matched comparisons between composed captions and decomposed counterparts built from the same underlying primitives. Evaluation uses a retrieval-style protocol with hard negatives designed to isolate errors in object recognition, attribute binding, and relation reasoning. On top of this setup, the paper defines two analyses. The first is the compositional integration gap: the performance difference between composed and decomposed captions under matched perturbations, intended to measure the cost of joint reasoning alone. The second is skill load: performance as a function of primitive counts, separated into self-load, where a skill degrades as more primitives of the same type are added, and cross-load, where it degrades as other primitive types increase. This is more informative than standard compositional benchmarks because it asks not only whether models fail at higher complexity, but why they fail.29:T511,The experiments cover 87K image-caption pairs for compositional integration analysis and 274K pairs for skill-load analysis, spanning object, attribute, and relation skills. The authors evaluate a diverse set of VLMs, including standard contrastive encoders, models explicitly trained for compositional reasoning, and non-contrastive architectures. The main findings are threefold. First, there is a consistent compositional integration cost: composed captions are harder than matched decomposed counterparts. However, this gap explains only part of the overall degradation as caption complexity increases. Second, each skill shows distinct sensitivity to primitive counts, and the degradation is not purely type-local. Models exhibit both self-load and cross-load effects, meaning that increasing relation primitives, for example, can also hurt object or attribute-related performance. Third, these patterns appear across model families, though with different magnitudes and shapes, suggesting that some architectures or training objectives may help with one bottleneck while leaving others untouched. The paper is strongest not in a single headline number, but in showing through controlled comparisons that compositional failure is a mixture of integration cost and skill-specific load effects.2a:Ta30,技术方案可以拆成四层。第一层是 Level-0 dense metric depth branch。作者采用 coarse-to-fine 的 DPT 风格解码器，融合视觉编码器的中间层特征和最终视觉 hidden states，输出全分辨率 metric depth。为了恢复绝对尺度，模型不只看 RGB，还显式输入相机内参。论文把内参编码成归一化 calibration、视场角、长宽比等紧凑描述，再通过 MLP 生成 FiLM 参数，对各尺度解码层做条件调制。这样做比把相机参数简单拼到语言 token 里更直接，因为 dense depth 分支需要的是数值尺度校准。另一个细节是高分辨率 RGB refinement pathway，用于补回空间压缩视觉 token 容易丢失的细边界、薄结构和深度突变。

第二层是 dense-to-language interface，这是全文最关键的创新。模型先基于引用框或 referring prompt 确定对象区域，再在这些区域内对预测深度和 decoder feature 做 pooling，形成 object-aligned continuous geometry tokens，并且显式绑定 object identifier。这里的设计重点不是把整张深度图塞给 LLM，而是把“哪个对象对应哪段几何证据”编码清楚，避免多对象问题里对象-数值错配。

第三层是几何信息保持机制。作者注意到，仅靠最终答案监督，geometry token 在进入语言上下文后很容易失去可恢复的 metric content。因此加入 auxiliary depth objectives，在 context interaction 前后都要求能从这些 token 中恢复对象级深度；同时加入 ranking objective，监督对象间深度顺序。这个设计很像给跨模态 bottleneck 加了信息守恒约束：不是只要求最后答对，而是要求中间表示仍然保留可读出的几何量。

第四层是 instance-level instruction tuning 与 cached continuation。模型把对象级几何 token 连同问题一起送入语言生成，但通过 cached continuation 避免对图像和问题反复编码，降低推理成本。训练任务按论文的层级划分覆盖 Level 1 的实例级测量和 Level 2 的组合推理，使模型不仅会报深度值，也会做区间筛选、相对比较、阈值判断等组合操作。

和已有工作相比，DepthLM 更偏向把深度预测吸收到语言建模里，没有显式 object-grounded evidence interface；DepthVLM 虽然也做 dense prediction 与文本联合，但这篇更强调“把模型自己的 dense metric prediction 变成对象级语言证据”，并通过前后双重监督保证数值信息在语言上下文中不被洗掉。这是它与一般 depth-aware VLM 的主要区别。2b:Ta18,The method has four layers. First is the Level-0 dense metric depth branch. The model uses a coarse-to-fine DPT-style decoder that fuses intermediate vision features with the final visual hidden states to predict full-resolution metric depth. To recover absolute scale, camera intrinsics are explicitly encoded into normalized calibration, field-of-view, and aspect-ratio descriptors, then mapped through an MLP into FiLM parameters that modulate every decoder scale. This is more targeted than simply appending camera values to language tokens, because the dense branch needs direct metric calibration. A high-resolution RGB refinement pathway is added to recover thin structures, sharp boundaries, and local detail that compressed visual tokens tend to lose.

Second is the dense-to-language interface, which is the central idea of the paper. Given object boxes or referring prompts, the model pools predicted depths and decoder features over each referenced region, producing object-aligned continuous geometry tokens tied to explicit object identifiers. The point is not to feed a whole depth map into the language model, but to preserve the mapping between object references and their geometric evidence.

Third is geometry preservation. The authors argue that answer supervision alone is too weak: once geometry tokens interact with the language context, their metric content can degrade. To prevent this, they add auxiliary depth objectives that require object-level depth to remain recoverable both before and after contextualization, plus a ranking loss that supervises relative depth ordering across objects. This effectively imposes an information-preservation constraint on the multimodal bottleneck.

Fourth is instance-level instruction tuning and cached continuation. The geometry tokens are injected into answer generation together with the question, while cached continuation avoids repeatedly re-encoding the image and prompt. Training covers both instance-level measurement and compositional reasoning, so the model learns not only to estimate depth values but also to use them in interval filtering, thresholding, and relational decisions.

Compared with prior work, DepthLM absorbs depth prediction into language modeling but does not provide an explicit object-grounded evidence interface. DepthVLM jointly handles dense prediction and text, but this paper goes further in making the model’s own dense metric output the direct evidence source for object-level reasoning, with explicit supervision to keep the numeric signal intact through language interaction.2c:T71e,The model size is 4B. Evaluation is organized into three levels. Level 0 measures dense metric depth prediction, where the paper reports the best average dense δ1 across nine datasets among the compared methods. This matters because the reasoning module depends on the quality of the underlying geometry. Level 1 evaluates instance-level metric depth estimation, asking whether the model can assign accurate depth to individual referenced objects. Level 2 evaluates compositional depth reasoning, where answers depend jointly on object identity, spatial relations, and numeric constraints. The paper also introduces the Depth-VQA benchmark, separating object measurement, relative reasoning, and metric-constrained reasoning. DepthEvidence achieves the highest overall reasoning accuracy on both reasoning tracks while keeping general VQA performance broadly stable relative to the base model.

The most useful part of the empirical story is the ablation logic. Camera-conditioned decoding improves metric-scale recovery. The high-resolution RGB pathway helps local structure and depth discontinuities. The dense-to-language interface is better suited than relying only on compressed visual tokens for object-level geometry binding. Geometry supervision before and after contextualization improves the recoverability of metric information from the intermediate tokens and translates into stronger Level-2 reasoning. The ranking loss helps relative-depth questions. The benchmark design is also important: many questions require both object filtering and numeric interval constraints, which makes it easier to distinguish vague spatial priors from genuine object-level metric reasoning. The paper is not just optimizing one metric; it connects dense prediction, object measurement, and final language answers in one chain.2d:T562,This one is worth a close read. Start with the introduction and the Level 0/1/2 task decomposition; otherwise it is easy to misread the paper as a standard depth-aware VLM. Then focus on Figure 2 and the method section: how camera intrinsics condition the dense decoder, how the dense-to-language interface extracts object-aligned geometry tokens, and why the auxiliary depth objectives are applied both before and after language-context interaction. In the experiments, read along a three-step question: is the geometry branch accurate, is object-level metric information preserved, and does the language model actually use it for reasoning? Prioritize the nine-dataset dense δ1 summary, the main Depth-VQA results, and the ablations removing geometry supervision, ranking loss, camera conditioning, and RGB refinement. If time allows, inspect the benchmark construction, especially metric-range, threshold, and gap-based questions, because those are the clearest tests of whether numeric content survives the language interface. A full careful read likely takes 60 to 90 minutes. The deeper follow-up questions are whether continuous geometry tokens generalize to 3D, video, or robotics, whether object-aligned evidence interfaces can emerge in larger native multimodal pretraining, and whether the same recoverability principle can be applied to other continuous modalities.2e:T4e1,The proposed symbiotic architecture replaces the usual thin adapter with an injector. In a standard audio language model, the audio encoder produces embeddings, the adapter maps them into the LLM embedding space, and the LLM performs prefilling over the full audio sequence. Here, the injector directly writes audio-conditioned key/value tensors into the short-term memory of the LLM. Since it generates a KV cache for every LLM layer, the injector has the same number of layers as the backbone, but it can be much narrower. The injector is built from CNN-style LConv blocks rather than vanilla Transformer blocks. The rationale is that audio-to-cache conversion mostly needs local context, while the frozen Transformer backbone can handle global integration during generation. The LConv block uses depthwise 1D convolution between pointwise dense layers, with RMSNorm replacing the normalization used in Conformer-style modules for better stability under small-batch training. The described configuration uses 2048-dimensional inputs to the depthwise convolution, kernel size 15, max-pooling subsampling with stride 8, and separate affine projections into key and value spaces. RMSNorm is applied to keys, and also to values for training stability.2f:T40d,LIRSeg introduces learnable latent tokens in place of explicit CoT. Training has two stages. The first stage performs supervised spatial alignment, grounding the latent tokens in object-relevant visual regions so that they encode spatial evidence rather than serving as generic placeholders. The second stage applies RL with segmentation rewards to refine these tokens under a compact token budget. Since latent tokens do not inherit the representational diversity of pretrained language tokens, the authors add three mechanisms to avoid collapse and weak exploration: extreme-advantage sampling to emphasize informative reward signals, decoupled exploration–stability updates to separate exploratory changes from representation-preserving updates, and latent diversity amplification to keep different latent tokens from converging to the same role. Compared with Seg-Zero, PixelThink, and DR2Seg, the key change is not shorter textual reasoning; it is the removal of textual reasoning in favor of visually grounded latent computation.30:T4dd,The method, called action forcing, starts from pixel displacement tracks across frames. Since similar egomotion directions induce recurring coherent motion patterns in the image plane, the authors run PCA over these displacement vectors and use the leading components as an egomotion basis. The first two component weights are used as low-dimensional action inputs. This avoids conditioning on dense flow or raw tracks at inference time, and it gives the action space an explicit affine structure: zero means no egomotion, scalar magnitude controls strength, sign controls direction, and axes can be combined. The world model is built by adapting a pretrained video DiT, specifically Wan2.1, under a conditional flow matching objective. The model learns a velocity field between noise latents and clean video latents, and the action is injected into the velocity network itself. This is different from applying a fixed transformation after generation; the action can shape the latent trajectory and the scene evolution. The paper also introduces an online latent egomotion critic that distills pixel-space motion into differentiable latent supervision, grounding action following without backpropagating through the video decoder or the tracker.31:T62b,The method has three main components. First, a physical mechanism interpreter maps each action-outcome branch to a distribution over latent mechanisms rather than a point estimate. This matters because different interventions constrain the underlying physics to different degrees: a strong push may sharply identify friction, while a weak interaction may leave many mechanisms plausible. Second, the paper introduces a shared-world evidence score. Instead of forcing branch posteriors to be identical, it measures whether all branches retain common support for a shared mechanism relative to an initial-scene prior p(z|I0). The prior correction is important: it prevents uninformative branches from appearing falsely consistent simply because they remain broad and uncertain. Third, the learned compatibility signal is injected into both training and sampling. The interpreter is trained using same-world groups versus mismatched-world groups, then frozen. Its evidence score is used during flow training and also as sampling-time guidance, so the generated rollouts are nudged toward sets of futures that admit one common physical explanation. A key design choice is weak coupling: the model does not force videos or latent posteriors to collapse to the same representation, only to remain jointly explainable by one mechanism. For evaluation, the paper defines the shared-world gap: fit physical parameters separately for each branch, then fit one shared parameter set jointly across branches; the extra error under the shared fit quantifies inconsistency across interventions.32:T454,Experiments are conducted in three controlled physical environments: Push Cube, Push Rope, and Pour Water, covering rigid-body, deformable, and particle-based dynamics. The main baseline is ACWM-DiT under the ACWM-Phys interaction setup. Relative to ACWM-DiT, OneWorld reduces the mean shared-world gap by 84.3%, which is the central result, and also improves mean M-MSE by about 7.7×10^-4, suggesting that consistency gains do not come at the expense of ordinary rollout quality. The most convincing part of the empirical section is the separation between single-branch plausibility and multi-branch physical compatibility: some baseline predictions fit physics reasonably well branch by branch, but fail badly once a single shared physical configuration is required. The paper also highlights the role of distributional mechanism inference, same-world versus mismatched-world interpreter training, and the use of the compatibility signal at both training and sampling time. The contribution is less about scale and more about closing the loop between formulation, training signal, and evaluation protocol.33:T734,The paper builds on OPD and OPSD. In standard OPD, the student samples on-policy trajectories and an external teacher provides next-token logits at each generated prefix. The student is trained by matching these dense token-level targets. OPSD removes the external teacher by using a second copy of the same initial checkpoint as a privileged teacher. The teacher sees the problem, the ground-truth solution, and the student-generated prefix; the student only sees the problem. This transfers gold-conditioned local guidance to the student.

The limitation is that prior OPSD keeps the teacher frozen. The authors probe fixed incorrect trajectories and show that a frozen gold-conditioned teacher often prefers EOS at incorrect endpoints rather than tokens that initiate reflection or correction. Meanwhile, the student’s tendency to reflect increases during training, making the frozen teacher less suitable for later on-policy prefixes.

Dynamic Co-Evolution addresses this by updating the privileged teacher across rounds. After each training update, the resulting checkpoint initializes both the next student and a detached gold-conditioned teacher. The teacher is still frozen within a round, which preserves a stable target, but it is refreshed between rounds so that revision behaviors learned by the student can become part of the next round’s supervision. This is more adaptive than a permanently frozen teacher and more stable than a fully online teacher.

Self-Refined Concise Learning addresses the cost side of stronger revision. More self-checking and backtracking can improve correctness but also inflate output length. SRCL trains on answer-verified, shorter rewrites of the same on-policy responses. DCE improves the ability to recover from mistakes; SRCL encourages the model to keep that ability while using fewer tokens.34:T5fe,The experiments use Qwen3 models at 8B, 4B, and 1.7B scale and evaluate Average@12 on four competition-level math reasoning benchmarks. Compared with OPSD, DCE improves Average@12 by 35.41, 37.15, and 12.98 percentage points at 8B, 4B, and 1.7B respectively. Matched-budget controls indicate that the gains are not explained by longer generations alone.

SRCL is most visible under constrained inference budgets. Under the lowest tested 8K evaluation budget, DCE+SRCL reaches 35.07% Average@12 with a mean of 7,500 output tokens per response. It outperforms the OPSD test-time-scaling control by 6.60 percentage points while generating about 692 fewer tokens per response.

The teacher-behavior probes are central. On fixed incorrect Qwen3-8B trajectories from AIME 2024, AIME 2025, and AIME 2026, the frozen gold-conditioned teacher assigns 94.5% average probability to EOS at incorrect endpoints and only 22.0% to observed reflection cues such as “Wait”. After DCE, the privileged teacher’s endpoint EOS probability drops from 90.4% to 41.3%, while its probability on observed reflection tokens rises from 32.8% to 77.4%. This shows that the local supervision changes direction: from stopping at a wrong endpoint toward checking and revising.

Ablations compare per-round teacher updates with frozen, EMA, and periodic alternatives. Updating the teacher every round performs best, suggesting that teacher refresh frequency is a substantive training variable in reasoning self-distillation rather than an implementation detail.35:T445,The central concept is strategic diversity: substantive variation in how a problem is approached, not lexical variation or embedding-space distance. The paper proposes two ways to generate strategically diverse self-training data using the model itself. The first adapts Verbalized Sampling: the model lists multiple possible approaches with estimated probabilities, and each approach is then used as a hidden instruction for generating a full response. The second method, Groot, asks the model to build a hierarchical decision tree over approaches and samples distinct root-to-leaf paths. This is natural for competitive programming, where solutions branch by algorithm, data structure, and implementation choices. For story planning, where narrative decisions are interdependent and less hierarchical, the unstructured VS-style approach is more suitable. Compared with high-temperature IID sampling, both methods force the model to commit to different approaches before producing the final answer, so they change the strategy distribution rather than only perturbing token-level realization.36:T86a,实验主干基于 Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct 与 PMC-VQA。SFT 使用 10k 训练题、1.5k 验证题、2k clean test，且作者检查了训练集与 clean test 在图像内容和 image-question 对上的无重叠。三个 SFT 配置的最佳 checkpoint 都出现在半个 epoch。最关键的 clean-test 结果是：仅语言侧 LoRA 的 M1-L，correct-image accuracy 只提升 +1.10 个百分点，95% paired bootstrap CI 为 -0.85 到 +3.05，统计上并不稳；但 visual-benefit events 减少 2.40 个点，image sensitivity 下降 5.60 个点，同时样本级别上出现 155 个新获得的视觉收益案例和 203 个丢失案例。这说明净准确率几乎不变时，底层决策机制已经明显重排。作者还发现扩大适配范围到 merger 或 vision encoder 并没有带来更好的 held-out 视觉效用，反而 clean-test accuracy 更低，说明在这个数据量和任务设置下，更多视觉参数可训练并不等于更好地学会用视觉。GRPO 部分，标准 answer-only GRPO 在 2000 prompt trajectory 上持续优化，但验证集选出的 M2-500 在 clean test 上几乎不改准确率，说明 reward 在被优化，但收益没有转移到最终任务指标。证据目标部分，M3 与 M4 的差异揭示了一个重要现象：如果用 canonical option token 来计分，得到的是一种“选项支持度”信号；如果沿真实生成 token path 计分，学到的则更接近实际解码行为。作者又做了 500/1000/2000 prompt exposure 的 dose-matched 比较，发现 evidence score 的提升里既有训练暴露量带来的 acquisition，也有从训练集到验证集的 transfer，而且不同 score component 的变化方向并不一致。换句话说，看到 evidence objective 变好，不能直接推断模型更会看图，还要问是哪个 token、哪一段路径、在哪个数据子集上变好。全文的 paired transition 分析是亮点，因为它把 aggregate 指标拆回到单题层面的 gained/lost visual-benefit cases，能看清楚模型是在“把原来依赖图像的题做成了不依赖图像也能答对”，还是“真的学会了更多视觉证据”。37:T56b,The paper has three technical layers. First, it defines a controlled evaluation protocol with three conditions per question: Correct image (C), NoImage (N), and Shuffled image (S). From these, it derives metrics such as visual-benefit rate (VBR), visual-harm rate (VHR), and image sensitivity (IS), which are more informative than plain accuracy because they test whether the answer depends on the image in the right way. Second, it compares different SFT adaptation scopes on Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct: M1-L applies LoRA only to language-model attention projections; M1-ML also trains the multimodal merger; M1-VML further adapts the vision encoder. This isolates whether opening more of the visual pathway actually improves held-out visual utility. Third, it studies RL-style objectives. Standard answer-only GRPO is initialized from M1-L to form M2. Then two evidence-objective variants are introduced: M3 uses canonical option-token scores, while M4 scores tokens along the actual generation path. This is an important implementation-level distinction. The paper shows that the meaning of the reward changes depending on which token path is scored, and it uses matched-dose comparisons to separate objective effects from simple training exposure effects. A supplementary SLAKE experiment further examines how native-answer token learning interacts with generation and stopping behavior.38:T6ef,The main experiments use Qwen2.5-VL-3B-Instruct on PMC-VQA, with 10k training questions, 1.5k validation questions, and a 2k clean test set with no image-content or image-question overlap with training. All selected SFT checkpoints occur at half an epoch. The most important result is that M1-L, the language-only LoRA SFT setup, changes correct-image accuracy by only +1.10 percentage points on the clean test, with a 95% paired bootstrap confidence interval of -0.85 to +3.05. Yet visual-benefit events decrease by 2.40 points and image sensitivity drops by 5.60 points. At the paired-example level, the model gains 155 visual-benefit cases but loses 203, showing substantial internal redistribution despite a small net accuracy change. Expanding adaptation to the merger or vision encoder does not help; broader multimodal adaptation lowers clean-test accuracy, suggesting that more trainable visual components do not automatically translate into better visual use under this data regime. For RL, standard answer-only GRPO continues to optimize its reward, but the validation-selected checkpoint M2-500 leaves clean-test accuracy nearly unchanged. The evidence-objective analysis is the most nuanced part: M3 and M4 behave differently because canonical option-token scoring and generation-path-aligned scoring capture different signals. Dose-matched comparisons at 500, 1000, and 2000 prompt exposures show that improvements in evidence scores mix together training-dose effects, score acquisition, and transfer, and that different score components move for different reasons. The paired transition analysis is especially useful because it reveals whether the model is truly learning to use visual evidence or merely becoming correct in a way that is less image-dependent.39:T522,The proposed method is Calibrated Importance Sampling, or CIS. Standard token-level correction uses the importance weight r = π_train(y_t|h_t) / π_infer(y_t|h_t). The paper argues that this ratio entangles two factors: the confidence of the sampled token and the actual discrepancy between the inference and training engines. It rewrites the ratio as a function of token confidence and an additive log-odds displacement ε_t. If the inference probability of the sampled token is p and the training engine shifts its log-odds by ε, the corrected probability can be written as sigmoid(logit(p)+ε), giving a ratio of roughly exp(ε)/(1-p+p exp(ε)). This shows why a fixed ratio threshold is poorly calibrated: the same ε produces a much larger ratio change for low-confidence tokens than for high-confidence tokens. CIS clips ε directly instead of clipping r. Since the measured ε distribution is approximately invariant across confidence buckets, a single threshold can be applied to the discrepancy component. When mapped back to the ratio, this becomes a confidence-dependent cap that is tighter for high-confidence tokens. The theoretical analysis shows that CIS bounds the second-moment term that can explode under exact importance correction, while introducing a bias controlled by the truncated excess.3a:T436,The analysis builds on the view that masked diffusion models minimize kinetic energy during denoising and follow near-geodesic paths from the masked state to the output distribution. The authors interpret alignment as creating an energy barrier between safe refusal outputs and harmful compliance outputs. Known attacks then fall into two groups. PAD and DIJA obscure the safety disposition at initialization by injecting structural connectors or interleaving harmful tokens with mask spans. Anchoring attacks intervene mid-trajectory by replacing model predictions with harmful tokens at a chosen denoising step and then remasking. From this view, the paper defines three training-free logit-space detectors. The first is a step-0 ratio between refusal-token probability mass and compliance-token probability mass. The other two are trajectory-velocity signals derived with a Fisher-metric decomposition, measuring kinetic energy in complementary subspaces. The refusal and compliance token sets can be derived automatically from held-out prompts, rather than manually curated.3b:T466,The main evaluation uses LLaDA-8B-Instruct and Dream-v0-Instruct-7B, with additional checks on LLaDA-1.5, LLaDA-MoE-7B-A1B-Instruct, Chinese prompts, and different generation and block lengths. The datasets include 400 HarmBench behaviors, 520 AdvBench behaviors, 200 AlpacaEval instructions, and 250 XSTest safe prompts. Each model is tested under seven conditions: clean, XSTest, direct harmful prompts, PAD, anchoring at step 1, anchoring at step 8, and DIJA. This gives 5,050 prompt-condition pairs per model and 10,100 pairs across the two main models. Generation uses temperature 0, greedy confidence-based remasking, response length 128, and one token decoded per step. The results show that the three signals capture different failure modes: the step-0 ratio is informative for template-style attacks, while velocity-based signals are useful for mid-trajectory interventions. In the reported stress tests, no tested attack configuration both evades all three signals and still produces harmful generations reliably. The comparison with DiffuGuard-SD is detection-focused, not a full generation-time defense comparison.3c:T78e,The proposed method, SCALPEL, is a contrastive sparse autoencoder designed to learn forget-selective features. The paper first formalizes an energy-bias view of reconstruction-based extraction. If activations decompose into multiple concept components, the marginal gain from improving reconstruction of component j scales with its energy. As a result, high-dispersion background concepts are favored over low-dispersion forget concepts. This is not presented as a full impossibility theorem, but as a useful incentive-level explanation for why standard extractors blend precise targets into broader features.

SCALPEL modifies the SAE objective with contrastive learning centered on forget anchors. Each forget example is augmented into paraphrases and rewritten variants, and different views of the same anchor are treated as positives. Negatives are sampled from retain data rather than from other forget examples. That design matters: using retain negatives helps separate target-specific structure from shared linguistic and task background, while avoiding the mistake of pushing related forget examples away from each other. After training, the SAE yields a fixed set of sparse forget-associated features. At inference time, the same feature set is available for every input, but each feature is subtracted only when activated by the encoder, producing an explicit and input-dependent internal filter.

Two details are especially important. First, the paper uses a norm-aware feature selection rule, because intervention strength depends both on encoder activation and on the magnitude of the decoder direction. Second, the theory section gives three supporting arguments: reconstruction induces energy bias, contrastive training favors target-selective sparse features, and high-scoring features admit a bound on expected background perturbation. These are best read as justification for the design, not as deletion guarantees.3d:T648,Experiments are run in the controlled TOFU author-level unlearning setting across three model families. The paper compares SCALPEL against mechanistic baselines such as PCA, NMF, and standard SAEs, as well as optimization-based unlearning methods. The evaluation is multi-axis: forgetting effectiveness, retain preservation, feature selectivity, and robustness of the removed trace.

The main empirical picture is consistent. First, at the representation-extraction level, SCALPEL learns features that are more concentrated on the forget target and less entangled with retain-side background structure than reconstruction-only methods. The paper supports this with activation analyses and feature-level diagnostics, including residual-stream studies on Qwen. Second, as an unlearning intervention, SCALPEL achieves competitive forgetting relative to optimization-based baselines while causing less collateral damage on retained knowledge. Third, the norm-aware feature ranking improves over activation-only ranking, confirming that decoder magnitude matters for practical intervention quality. Fourth, ablations show that the contrastive setup is sensitive to positive/negative construction: treating other forget examples as negatives hurts selectivity because it breaks shared target structure.

The evidence is strongest in the controlled benchmark setting. The authors are explicit that this is not certified deletion and not a full solution to GDPR-style erasure. The method removes extractable internal traces, but that does not imply all target-related information has disappeared from the parameters.3e:T4de,Start with the introduction and Section 3. The energy-bias argument is the conceptual backbone of the paper; once that is clear, the SCALPEL design choices make sense. In the method section, focus on the augmentation scheme, the contrastive objective, the sparse coding setup, and the inference-time feature subtraction rule. The key design question is why negatives come from retain data rather than other forget examples. In the theory section, do not get stuck in every derivation; instead track the three main claims: why reconstruction favors background, how contrastive learning improves selectivity, and under what assumptions the background-perturbation bound holds. In experiments, read the main comparison tables first, then the ablations on norm-aware selection and contrastive sample construction.

A 30-minute skim is enough for the main method and findings. If reading more closely, the residual-stream analysis on Qwen around layer -4 is worth attention because it most directly illustrates the low-energy-target versus high-energy-background story. The main open question to keep in mind is whether this representation-level filtering remains stable on larger models, broader knowledge domains, and stronger adversarial prompting.3f:T734,The method is a six-stage circuit-discovery pipeline. They start from 21 hand-written neutral prompts in single languages and define the objective around the target language probability at the first generated token. They then introduce LIHA, a head-level attribution score computed via activation patching between a clean target-language prompt and a corrupted foil-language prompt. This identifies attention heads whose activations causally push the model toward the correct response language.

Next, they run Edge Attribution Patching over source-head to destination-layer edges. EAP uses a first-order Taylor approximation to estimate the effect of replacing an upstream clean activation with its corrupted counterpart, allowing many edges to be scored with one backward pass per sample. Because this is only an approximation, they cap the candidate set at 2,000 edges per model and then run exact activation patching on each candidate edge. Only edges with sign agreement between EAP and exact patching are retained. The surviving edges are assembled into a DAG, interpreted as the verified first-token broadcasting circuit. Finally, they validate the graph with boundary ablations: removing in-graph edges should hurt language identification, while removing out-of-graph edges should not.

The structural findings are more interesting than the raw scores. Four standalone models show a recurring deep-layer hub topology, where a small number of late heads appear to broadcast language identity forward. The Pythia models also show more explicit multi-hop routing chains. By contrast, Qwen2.5-1.5B Base and Instruct fail the same necessity/irrelevance separation and also have the weakest EAP-to-exact correlation. The authors interpret this conservatively as a pipeline-reliability issue rather than evidence that Qwen lacks such circuits.40:T505,The study covers six models from four families, roughly 560M to 2.8B parameters: GPT-2, BLOOM-560M, Pythia-1B, Pythia-2.8B, and Qwen2.5-1.5B Base/Instruct. The edge search space grows with model size, from 792 scored edges in GPT-2 to 15,872 in Pythia-2.8B. For tractability, every model is truncated to a 2,000-edge candidate pool before exact verification. After sign-consistent exact patching, final graph sizes range from 816 edges for GPT-2 to 1,016 for Qwen2.5-1.5B.

The most important empirical result is not task performance but validation quality. The paper reports EAP-to-exact-patching correlations as a sanity check on the attribution stage. The four standalone models show stronger agreement and cleaner boundary-ablation behavior, which makes their extracted graphs more credible as functional circuits. The Qwen pair shows weaker correlation and weaker necessity/irrelevance separation, so those graphs should be treated with caution. Another useful aspect is that the analysis goes beyond listing important heads: it verifies head-to-layer edges, which is closer to a causal routing account.

That said, the dataset is tiny, only 21 prompts, so the claims should be read as a careful case study rather than a broad statistical statement about multilingual pretraining.41:T597,The framework is built on finite structural causal models. The evaluator has access to the full state ξ=(X,H), while the learner may not observe the latent context H. For a query z=(ξ,a), the evaluator defines a structural index σ_M(ξ,a), which records the active context class, the directly affected variable, the value class, and the first structural response delay. A mechanism memory μ is a finite set of tuples θ=(a,c,y,v,δ), and the answer map T_μ(z) is evaluated against the ground-truth mechanism map. The exact mechanism risk R_Q measures full answer equivalence under a fixed probe distribution Q; coordinate risks decompose errors over context, target, value, and delay, while missed and spurious mechanism risks give operational diagnostics. The main theoretical result expresses optimal probe error as a Bayes decision risk and shows that zero error is possible exactly when each learning-interface fiber lies inside a single probe-answer fiber. The adaptation setup introduces a schema map φ from source to target memory, stable and shifted index sets, and losses L_stab and L_shift. Pure transfer fails on shifted mechanisms, while broad relearning can corrupt stable ones. The proposed Causal Core mechanism uses evidence-gated writing and filtered readout, with gates estimated from paired action/reference interventions or propensity-corrected observational data under standard identification conditions.42:T490,The model abstraction starts with an unobserved physical state x, an observation o=h(x), a fixed language instruction, and an internal representation λ(x) produced by the VLA backbone. A stochastic policy π then generates a continuous-time open-loop action chunk u(t), which is treated as the control input to a nonlinear control-affine dynamical system. Quantities of interest are defined along the controlled trajectory rather than as static labels. For probing, the work constructs an ideal representation in which multiple physical quantities and their propagated values under candidate action trajectories admit shared linear readouts. For steering, it introduces a path-signature-based exponential-family policy. Path signatures summarize continuous action paths through iterated-integral features, and the exponential-family geometry yields a dually flat statistical manifold. This provides a formal link between linear probes in representation space and linear steering of action-trajectory distributions. The key difference from standard LLM LRH is that the represented direction is tied to future dynamics and policy geometry, not only to a static concept.43:T428,The authors define a growing heterogeneous manifold-mixture model where the number of components, weights, reach, separation, and perturbation scales may change with sample size. Each component can have its own intrinsic dimension and smoothness. They first prove an aggregate minimax lower bound, denoted R_n, which accounts for each component’s difficulty through dimension, smoothness, and effective sample size. They then construct an oracle tangent local-polynomial estimator: it estimates local charts and tangent coordinates, and performs reduced-dimensional polynomial regression in those coordinates. The oracle supplies dimension, smoothness, and bandwidth, but not the tangent projector or the task function. A structure-informed transformer is then built using softmax attention and ReLU feed-forward networks to implement geometric preconditioning, chartwise selection, and local reduced regression in one forward pass. The final analysis separates near-ERM risk into in-context error, approximation error, finite-task error, and optimization error.44:T4a1,TalkMesh places agents on a sparse communication graph. Each agent uses a frozen base model M with two LoRA adapters: a confidence head V and a talk policy T. Inference has five stages. First, agents independently sample proposals, matching the independence assumption of self-consistency. Second, V scores each proposal using the sigmoid of the Yes-versus-No logit gap under a fixed verification prompt. Third, the highest-confidence agent becomes the speaker and emits a hint capped at 112 tokens. Fourth, listeners whose confidence is below 0.5, or who did not produce an answer, revise their proposals using the hint. A revision is accepted only if V scores it higher than the original. Fifth, agents run gossip consensus to approximate a centralized confidence-weighted vote without a coordinator. The confidence head is trained as an outcome verifier. The talk policy is trained inside the protocol with GRPO using a flip reward: +1 when a revision changes a wrong answer to a correct one, and -1 for the reverse. The speaker receives the mean flip reward over its listeners. Training uses a curriculum over rollout size K = 3, 5, and 8, while evaluation scales to larger meshes.45:T402,The main experiments compare self-consistency, confidence reranking without messages, and the trained mesh. With three agents, the trained mesh reaches the accuracy of self-consistency with 32 samples. At N = 32, SmolLM3-3B on MATH-500 improves from 0.492 under self-consistency to 0.722 with TalkMesh, while reranking without messages reaches 0.590. Qwen3.5-0.8B on GSM8K improves from 0.568 to 0.705, with reranking at 0.655. In an attack setting with N = 8 and four compromised agents submitting the same wrong answer with claimed confidence 0.99, majority vote drops to 0.000 on 150 test problems. The defended mesh, which rescores solutions and gates revisions, retains 0.507 on Qwen3.5-0.8B GSM8K versus 0.667 without attack; an undefended version drops to at most 0.007. Additional evaluations cover SwarmBench and traffic control. On the severe traffic subset, messages reduce person delay for Qwen3.5-0.8B, 2B, and 4B meshes by 23.6±6.7 seconds; over all 70 scenarios, the 4B mesh reduces delay by 11.4±6.9 seconds.46:T423,The starting point is Karpathy’s AutoResearch loop: an LLM edits training code, launches an experiment, and keeps changes that improve a held-out metric. The authors deploy this pattern in two production representation-learning systems at Amazon and identify five recurring failure modes: infrastructure fragility, agent memory decay, search-direction stagnation, iteration-cost asymmetry, and metric fixation. The proposed scaffolding follows three principles. Prevent uses a semantic gate before execution, which is worthwhile when a failed run wastes many GPU-hours. Persist stores durable cross-job memory, so the agent does not repeat known mistakes or forget prior findings during multi-week campaigns. Redirect triggers strategic reframing when local code or hyperparameter edits stop producing progress. The implementation is a three-agent extension of AutoResearch. Recent empirical and theoretical study suggest that the training loss landscape often exhibits a \"river-valley\" structure, which features a low-loss manifold (river) flanked by sharp orthogonal directions with higher loss (mountains). In the long term, the optimization progress is determined primarily by the progress along the river. Wi","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-25T08:09:59Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2609_30957.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","math.OC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作要回答的是：在大模型预训练常见的“river-valley”损失地形里，momentum 到底为什么有用，以及它是通过什么机制带来加速。论文不是沿用经典凸二次函数里“加快线性收敛”的解释，而是把问题放到一个更贴近预训练动力学的场景里：参数主要沿低曲率、低损失的 river 前进，但同时会在正交的高曲率 valley 方向发生振荡。真正决定长期优化进度的是沿 river 的位移，而不是瞬时 loss 的高低。作者要解释的核心现象是：为什么带 momentum 的方法能稳定承受更大的学习率，从而更快沿 river 前进；以及为什么这类加速在训练曲线上常常表现为前期 loss 更高、后期在 sharp decay 后突然显出更低终点。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要有三层。第一，它给 WSD 一类训练 recipe 中“长时间大步长 + 末端急剧衰减”为何有效提供了更细的理论支撑，而且把 momentum 放进了同一个解释框架里。第二，它把 heavy-ball 在二次函数上的稳定性增益因子 (1+β)/(1-β) 推广到了非凸、且特征子空间会随轨迹旋转的 river-valley 地形，这一点对理解 Adam、Muon 这类带动量优化器很关键。第三，它强调一个常被训练曲线掩盖的事实：高学习率或高动量下看到的较高 loss，可能主要来自 valley 方向的振荡，而不是 river 方向进展变慢。对做预训练 recipe、scheduler、稳定性分析的人，这个视角是有直接操作意义的。贡献类型更偏“理论机制澄清”，不是新优化器，但它在解释现有大模型训练经验时有较强穿透力。","technical_approach":"技术上，论文研究的是 heavy-ball momentum 的离散迭代，在 river-valley 模型下分析其稳定性与沿 river 的推进速度。关键设定是把局部几何拆成两部分：一条低损失、低曲率、缓慢弯曲的 river manifold，以及与之正交的高曲率 valley 方向。优化难点不在于是否能下降，而在于能否在不被高曲率方向振荡放大的前提下，用足够大的步长快速沿 river 走。\n\n核心结论有两个。其一，momentum 允许的最大稳定学习率，相比 vanilla GD，领先阶上放大约 (1+β)/(1-β) 倍。这个因子在经典二次模型里早就出现过，但这里难点在于非凸情形下 river/valley 的方向会随位置旋转，梯度和动量在两个子空间之间会相互泄漏，不能直接套用固定特征分解。作者为此建立了新的投影分析和归纳框架，分别跟踪梯度项与动量项在 river 与 valley 两个方向上的量级，并控制它们在迭代中的耦合传播。\n\n其二，在“flat and slow-spinning river”的条件下，momentum 本身并不会显著改变沿 river 的单位步推进规律；真正的加速主要来自它把可容忍学习率上限抬高了。也就是说，momentum 更像一个稳定器，而不是直接在 river 方向制造额外加速度。这个判断很重要，因为它把“动量为什么快”从一个模糊的经验结论，拆成了“允许更激进的 η”与“river 上的内在动力学”两个部分。\n\n论文还给出一个配套结果：借助新的归纳技巧，作者顺带改进了 vanilla GD 在同类 river-valley 模型下的最大可容忍学习率界。这说明他们的分析不只是给 momentum 打补丁，而是对整个 river-valley 理论框架的稳定性边界都做了收紧。","experiments":"从给出的全文内容看，这篇工作的主轴是理论分析，实验更多承担现象对齐和直觉验证，而不是大规模 recipe benchmark。摘要与引言里明确对应的经验现象包括：WSD 在长时间保持较大学习率时，训练 loss 可能高于 cosine，但这是 valley 振荡抬高了表观 loss；在末端 sharp decay 后，振荡被压制，之前沿 river 累积的真实进展才显现出来，因此最终 loss 更低。论文还提到用合成 loss function 可视化 river-valley 地形，并与 Wen et al. 2025 对 LLM pretraining loss landscape 的 probing 结果呼应。\n\n就可提炼的实验信息而言，最重要的不是某个绝对数值，而是三类验证：1）在合成 river-valley 地形上，带 momentum 的迭代能在更大学习率下保持稳定，并更快沿 river 前进；2）理论预测的稳定阈值放大因子与实际可运行区间相符；3）WSD/大步长训练曲线中的“高 loss 但更好终点”可以用 river 进展与 valley 振荡分离来解释。当前提供的正文片段没有给出完整表格、参数量、数据规模或具体提升数字，因此不能编造具体数值。若精读原文，建议重点补齐 theorem 对应的数值实验、稳定边界图、以及与 vanilla GD 的阈值对比图。","reading_guide":"建议按“现象—模型—定理—证明技巧”这条线读。\n\n先看 Introduction，把作者要解释的训练现象抓牢：为什么大步长和 sharp decay 有时比平滑 cosine 更好，以及这里面 momentum 扮演什么角色。然后直接看 Theorem 4.1，确认最核心的结论：最大稳定学习率被放大到约 (1+β)/(1-β) 倍。这个定理是全文的支点。\n\n接着看 Lemma 5.1 及其前后的证明结构。这里最值得深挖，因为真正的新东西不只是结论，而是如何在 river/valley 子空间会旋转、且 gradient 与 momentum 互相串扰的情况下做归纳控制。读的时候重点盯三件事：1）river 与 valley 投影量是如何定义和更新的；2）哪些假设对应“flat”与“slow-spinning”；3）误差项如何累积、又如何被稳定性条件压住。\n\n如果时间有限，可以把证明细节分层处理：先读主定理陈述、关键引理和 proof sketch，再回头看技术性最强的归纳步骤。最后看关于 vanilla GD 学习率界改进的 remark，这部分能帮助判断作者的方法是否具有更一般的分析价值。\n\n预计精读时间 1.5 到 2.5 小时。做优化与训练稳定性的读者，值得把公式和图一起对照着看；只关心 recipe 启发的读者，读引言、主定理、实验图和讨论部分就够了。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks a precise question: in the river-valley loss geometry observed in LLM pretraining, why does momentum help, and what exactly is being accelerated? Instead of relying on the classical quadratic-story of faster linear convergence, it studies a more realistic regime where optimization mainly progresses along a low-loss, low-curvature river, while oscillating across sharp orthogonal valley directions. Long-run progress is governed by motion along the river, not by the instantaneous loss value. The target phenomenon is the one practitioners already see in training: momentum-based methods can tolerate larger learning rates, move faster in the useful direction, yet display higher apparent loss early on because of vertical oscillations.","importance":"Its value for pretraining research is mostly conceptual but quite concrete. First, it gives a cleaner explanation for why recipes such as WSD can benefit from a long high-LR phase followed by a sharp decay, and it places momentum inside the same picture. Second, it extends the classical heavy-ball stability gain factor, (1+β)/(1-β), from fixed quadratic objectives to a non-convex river-valley landscape where flat and sharp eigenspaces rotate along the trajectory. That matters for interpreting modern momentum-based optimizers, not just vanilla heavy-ball. Third, it sharpens an important practical distinction: a higher training loss under aggressive optimization may reflect oscillation in sharp directions rather than weaker progress in the task-relevant low-curvature direction. For people working on optimizer design, scheduler design, and training diagnostics, that is a useful lens rather than a purely formal result.","technical_approach":"$d","experiments":"$e","reading_guide":"$f","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇会直接影响你怎么看 WSD 和大 LR 稳态段；建议先复现其 river-valley 分解，再拿内部训练日志验证“高 loss 但沿河前进”是否真发生。","en":"This can change how you think about WSD and long high-LR plateaus; reproduce the river-valley decomposition first, then test on your own logs whether 'high apparent loss but real river progress' actually holds."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Miao Lu","Zeyu Bian","Kaiyue Wen","Beining Wu","Siyu Chen","Tianhao Wang","Zhiyuan Li"],"notable_authors":["Zhiyuan Li"],"tags":["optimization","momentum","learning-rate-schedule","training-dynamics","WSD"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.30957","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.30957","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.30342","title":"Low-Rank Friction for Memory-Efficient Transformer Pretraining","abstract":"iKFAD is a recently proposed optimiser that replaces adaptive learning rates with adaptive friction in the momentum dynamics, yet performs as well as Adam. Its limitation is that the full friction tensor $ξ\\in\\mathbb{R}^{m\\times n}$ carries the same $\\mathcal{O}(mn)$ memory overhead per layer as Adam's second-moment buffer. Here we replace iKFAD's friction tensor $ξ$ with a rank-1 outer-product factorisation built from row and column momentum statistics, resulting in Rank-1 iKFAD (R-iKFAD). This","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-24T11:53:47Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2609_30342.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["stat.ML","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"论文要解决的是 iKFAD 优化器的状态内存问题：iKFAD 用逐参数 friction tensor 替代 Adam 的二阶矩自适应缩放，性能可接近 Adam，但 friction tensor 仍然是 O(N) 级别，整体辅助状态没有比 Adam 少。","importance":"对预训练研究的启发主要在优化器状态压缩。它不是提出一个明显更强的训练 recipe，而是把 Adafactor 的低秩思想迁移到 friction-based optimizer 上。这个方向适合关注大模型预训练内存预算的人泛读，尤其是当 optimizer state 成为 ZeRO、FSDP、checkpointing 之外的瓶颈时。贡献量级偏方法增量：目标是保持 iKFAD 性能，同时降低 friction 状态，而不是改善收敛速度或 scaling 行为。","technical_approach":"方法叫 Rank-1 iKFAD。iKFAD 原来维护完整 friction tensor ξ，R-iKFAD 将 ξ 写成由行统计和列统计构造的 rank-1 近似，形式上类似 Adafactor 对 Adam 二阶矩 V 的分解。区别在于 Adafactor 聚合的是 squared gradients，并在 position update 中做缩放；R-iKFAD 聚合的是 squared momenta，并把自适应项放在 momentum dynamics 的 damping/friction 里。这样每个矩阵层的 friction 状态从 O(mn) 降到 O(m+n)，同时保留完整 momentum P。离散化部分采用 Hamiltonian/ODE 视角，将更新分成 position drift、gradient kick、linear friction、momentum-friction coupling 等子流，前三个子流可解析积分，耦合项用对称 freeze-and-update scheme 做半解析积分。这个设计的重点是稳定性，而不是简单把连续方程用 Euler 离散。","experiments":"论文报告 R-iKFAD 在 GPT-2 类 Transformer 预训练实验中与完整 iKFAD 基本保持性能一致，并显著减少 friction 相关状态。按方法定义，iKFAD 与 Adam 一样需要 momentum 加一个 O(N) 自适应状态；R-iKFAD 保留 N 个 momentum 状态，但把 friction 从每层 O(mn) 降到 O(m+n)，因此相对 iKFAD 约减少一半 optimizer auxiliary state。实验重点应理解为 parity check：压缩 friction 后是否破坏训练，而不是新的收敛优势。给出的全文片段没有包含完整表格数字，因此不应过度解读具体提升幅度。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 20–30 分钟。重点看 Introduction 里的四象限对照图，先明确 Adam、Adafactor、iKFAD、R-iKFAD 各自压缩的是哪个状态。然后看 Methodology 中连续时间方程，尤其是 squared gradients 与 squared momenta 的差别，以及 friction 进入 momentum equation 的方式。离散化小节只需抓住 splitting 的目的：避免直接离散导致的数值不稳。实验部分重点看训练 loss 曲线、optimizer state 统计和是否有 loss spike；如果没有大规模模型结果，可以把它当作优化器状态压缩的概念验证。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets the memory cost of iKFAD. iKFAD replaces Adam-style adaptive learning rates with adaptive friction in the momentum dynamics, but its full per-parameter friction tensor still costs O(N) memory, leaving the total auxiliary state comparable to Adam.","importance":"The useful signal for pretraining is optimizer-state compression. The paper does not claim a stronger training recipe; it asks whether the factored-state idea behind Adafactor can be applied to a friction-based optimizer without losing training quality. This is relevant when optimizer memory is a first-order constraint in large-model pretraining, alongside sharding and activation checkpointing. The contribution is incremental but technically clean: preserve iKFAD-like behavior while reducing the friction state.","technical_approach":"Rank-1 iKFAD factorizes the friction matrix using row and column momentum statistics. This mirrors Adafactor’s factorization of Adam’s second moment, but the object being compressed is different. Adafactor tracks squared gradients and applies the estimate in the position update; R-iKFAD tracks squared momenta and applies the estimate as adaptive damping in the momentum equation. For a matrix parameter of shape m by n, the friction state drops from O(mn) to O(m+n), while the full momentum buffer is retained. The discrete algorithm is derived from an ODE/Hamiltonian view and uses operator splitting: position drift, gradient kick, linear friction, and a coupled momentum-friction update. The coupled part is handled with a symmetric semi-analytic freeze-and-update step for numerical stability.","experiments":"The reported Transformer pretraining experiments, based on GPT-2-style models, show near-parity between R-iKFAD and full iKFAD while reducing the friction-related memory footprint. By construction, the auxiliary state changes from momentum plus full friction to momentum plus row/column friction factors, roughly halving the optimizer state relative to iKFAD or Adam-like two-state optimizers for large matrices. The available text does not expose the full numeric tables, so the safe reading is that the paper establishes a parity result rather than a convergence improvement.","reading_guide":"A 20–30 minute skim is enough. The numerical integration section is worth a quick pass because it explains why the method is not just a naive factored update. The key design choice is to hold the model family, information available at each decision date, prompt, output format, candidate universe, and portfolio construction fixed, and only intervene on reasoning effort. The measured object is not whether the response looks more thoughtful, but whether the induced stock scores, holdings, and net returns improve after costs.","importance":"The main relevance for pretraining and inference-scaling research is cautionary. Test-time compute gains observed on math or coding benchmarks rely on relatively clean correctness signals and often on verifiers. Trading has delayed, noisy feedback and a downstream decision rule that can attenuate or reverse score changes. The study shows that extra reasoning can change model behavior, portfolio turnover, output validity, and operating cost without yielding reproducible utility gains. This is useful when designing evaluations for reasoning models outside verifiable domains.","technical_approach":"The study uses a controlled intervention on reasoning level. On each formation date, a model scores the same 100 liquid U.S. equities under one of three input conditions: numerical features with identifiers removed, identifiable news, and masked news with company-identifying details removed. Scores are mapped into the same portfolio rule: long the top 10 stocks, short the bottom 10 stocks, equal-weighted and market-neutral. Each selection is held for five days, producing overlapping formation cohorts. Trading incurs 10 bps whenever capital is bought or sold. The main statistical unit is the paired daily portfolio return difference, not the individual asset prediction. Inference uses a five-lag Newey-West correction. Invalid responses are retained under an intention-to-treat rule and assigned a neutral score of zero. A frozen audit repeats identical tasks on 48 fixed formation dates three times per backbone to test generation-level reproducibility.","experiments":"The evaluation spans 241 formation dates in 2024, 100 U.S. equities, three model families from DeepSeek, GPT, and Gemini, and 808,800 asset-level predictions. The common comparison raises reasoning from baseline to low; DeepSeek also has a four-level curve over none, low, high, and maximum reasoning. None of the nine annual low-versus-baseline tests establishes a material improvement above the predeclared threshold of 0.25 bps/day. In the frozen audit, DeepSeek with masked news shows a low-versus-none return difference of -9.016 bps/day, with a 95% confidence interval of [-16.248, -1.784]. DeepSeek’s response across reasoning levels is non-monotonic. GPT and Gemini each show sign changes across repeated generations in at least one news condition. Output-format reliability also changes with reasoning, but the direction varies by model.","reading_guide":"A useful skim takes about 20–30 minutes. verifier、self-consistency、debate 数据，以及如何评价模型的“可组合性”。","technical_approach":"作者引入 Steiner 的群体任务分类，把常见多智能体评测按组合规则划分，而不是按 benchmark 名称划分。重点分析两类：disjunctive task 和 compensatory task。前者包括问答、数学、选择题等，团队输出依赖至少一个成员答对、plurality voting、verifier selection 或 debate；后者包括 Fermi estimate 等连续数值估计，团队输出依赖平均或中位数。理论上，作者把独立采样的多个 agent 建模为在给定 item 后条件独立。这个假设推出两个大团队极限：plurality voting 会收敛到模型对该 item 的众数答案；averaging 会收敛到模型在该 item 上的系统性偏差。这个框架把“采样能否扩展”拆成两个问题：模型答案分布里是否存在正确答案，以及错误是否能相互抵消。","experiments":"实验覆盖 13 个 open-weight 模型，团队规模最高到 30。离散任务上，pass@N 随团队规模稳定上升，至少一个 agent 答对的概率提升约 5–20 个百分点；但 plurality voting 的收益受到众数答案限制，条件独立模型对其大团队表现的预测误差在 0.5 个百分点以内。revision round 能提高准确率，但 1 个 peer 和 29 个 peer 带来的提升接近，说明讨论轮的收益更多来自一次外部扰动，而不是团队规模本身。补偿型 Fermi 估计任务上，item-level bias 解释了约 87% 的 squared log-error，平均只能降低约 6% 的误差；这说明误差主要不是独立噪声，而是同一题上的共同偏差。异构团队能显著超过平均成员，但在离散任务上通常不能超过最强成员；在 Fermi 估计上，5 个 7B–8B 模型组成的团队可以超过单个成员。","reading_guide":"泛读即可，预计 25–35 分钟。重点看 Section 2 的 Steiner taxonomy 映射和大团队极限推导，这是主要增量。Section 3.2 可快速看，用来判断条件独立和多样性假设是否站得住。Section 3.3 的 disjunctive task 结果适合和 self-consistency、best-of-N、debate 放在一起读；关键是区分 pass@N 与 plurality voting。Section 3.4 的 Fermi estimate 分析更有信息量，尤其是 item-level bias 占 87% squared log-error 这一点，它解释了为什么平均多个模型并不一定接近 Galton 式 crowd wisdom。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper asks why larger LLM agent teams do not improve uniformly. The central issue is task structure. A team behaves differently when success depends on one member finding the right discrete answer than when success depends on averaging continuous estimates. Voting, pass@N, revision, and averaging have different statistical limits, so a single team-size scaling story is misleading.","importance":"The contribution is not a pretraining method, but it is useful for researchers working on test-time compute and model-output distributions. It shows that sampling more trajectories should not be interpreted as a direct gain in model capability. If errors are correlated, plurality voting converges to the model’s modal answer. If item-level bias dominates, averaging does little. These observations matter for designing self-consistency, debate data, verifiers, and evaluations of whether models provide genuinely diverse hypotheses.","technical_approach":"The authors use Steiner’s taxonomy of group tasks as the organizing frame. They focus on disjunctive tasks, such as QA and math problems where the team may succeed if one member is correct or if voting/verifier selection picks the right answer, and compensatory tasks, such as Fermi estimates where the team output is an average. They model independently sampled agents as conditionally independent given the item. Under this model, plurality voting converges to the model’s item-level modal answer, while averaging converges to the model’s item-level bias. This separates two mechanisms that are often conflated: the probability that the correct answer appears somewhere in the sample set, and the probability that aggregation selects it.","experiments":"The study evaluates 13 open-weight models with teams of up to 30 agents. On disjunctive tasks, pass@N increases with team size, with gains of roughly 5–20 percentage points. Plurality voting, however, is tightly bounded by the modal-answer limit; the conditional-independence model predicts large-team voting accuracy within about 0.5 points. Revision rounds improve accuracy, but using one peer gives nearly the same improvement as using 29 peers, suggesting that most of the benefit comes from a single perturbation rather than from scaling the team. On compensatory Fermi-estimation tasks, item-level bias explains about 87% of squared log-error, and averaging reduces error by only about 6%. Heterogeneous teams improve over their average member, but on disjunctive tasks they do not exceed the strongest member; for Fermi estimates, a five-model team of 7B–8B models can outperform its individual members.","reading_guide":"A skim is sufficient, about 25–35 minutes. Section 2 is the key part: it defines the task taxonomy and derives the large-team limits. Section 3.2 is useful for checking the independence and diversity assumptions. Section 3.3 is most relevant if you care about self-consistency, best-of-N, and debate; pay attention to the distinction between pass@N and plurality voting. Section 3.4 is the most informative empirical section, especially the finding that item-level bias accounts for 87% of squared log-error.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"读理论模型和团队规模曲线；它会降低对“多采样投票自然变强”的先验信心。","en":"Read the scaling model and team-size curves; it should reduce confidence in the prior that more sampled agents plus voting naturally improves performance."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Carolina Fortuna","Blaz Bertalanic"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["agent-scaling","test-time-compute","multi-agent","voting","task-structure"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31563","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.31563","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.30634","title":"In-Context Binding Capacity in Language Models","abstract":"How many assignments can a language model recall before it loses track of which value belongs to which entity? can keep track of in context before retrieval breaks down. The task is deliberately narrow: the model reads K assignments, passes through a distractor block, and must output the value bound to a queried entity. It isolates static binding recall rather than claiming to measure world-state tracking or multi-step state updates.","importance":"The useful contribution for pretraining research is a concrete capacity metric for in-context working memory, together with a careful separation between scale, recipe, interference, and task-specific learnability. On continuous recall curves for 12 models at or below 3B parameters, the measured half-recall load follows K50 = c N^0.820 with R² = 0.73. This is not a language-model-loss scaling law; it is an empirical law for zero-shot binding capacity. The broader sweep over 30 open models up to 12B shows large recipe-associated variation: for example, the Pythia ladder stays around k* = 3 to 6 from 410M to 12B, while Qwen2.5-3B reaches the measurement ceiling. The paper is appropriately cautious: in the continuous-curve subset, no clear recipe effect is detected after controlling for scale, partly because the number of modern models is limited.","technical_approach":"The task presents bindings of the form “entity: obligation”, where entities and one-token obligations are sampled from disjoint pools. After D distractor tokens, the model is queried with the entity and is counted correct if the correct obligation has the highest logit. Chance accuracy is 1 over the obligation pool, reported as 0.040. The discrete statistic k* is the smallest K where recall falls below the midpoint between the model’s own K=1 ceiling and chance. Because such thresholds can create artificial discontinuities, the paper also fits smooth recall-versus-log-K curves and reports K50, the continuous half-retention point. A separate interference analysis shows that distractors can reduce the single-binding ceiling without changing the load-dependent profile, which matters when comparing normalized curves across models.","experiments":"The threshold sweep covers 30 open models from ten families, ranging from GPT-2, OPT, BLOOM, Pythia, and Cerebras models to OLMo, Qwen2.5, Mistral, OLMoE, and DeepSeek-Coder variants. Pythia-70M is excluded because its K=1 accuracy is only 0.070 against 0.040 chance, making its threshold unreliable. After exclusion, k* ranges from 3 to 24, an 8x span. OLMo-1-7B, Qwen2.5-3B, and OPT-6.7B hit the k*=24 ceiling and are treated as right-censored. The continuous remeasurement covers 12 models at or below 3B and yields the N^0.820 fit. The paper also reports that direct task training can produce a factor-26 gain relative to an extrapolated zero-shot law, while emphasizing that this comparison mixes different estimands and extrapolates outside the original load range.","reading_guide":"A focused skim should take about 20–30 minutes. Many efficient alternatives address this cost by deciding in advance where attention should be sparse or local. We argue that attention approximation should instead be approached as a geometric problem, with the relevant interaction geometry learned from data: natural-language dependencies are input-dependent and difficult to prescribe in advance, so the model should learn where posi","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-25T13:38:28Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2609_31261.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是长上下文自注意力里一个很具体但常被简化的问题：哪些 query-key 交互应该保留，哪些可以衰减甚至近似掉，不应由人工预先写死，而应由模型根据输入语义和位置共同决定。现有高效注意力大多先验规定局部窗口、块稀疏或固定模式，代价是把“相关性几何”假定成统一规则；而标准 RoPE 虽然提供了相对位置结构，却不直接表达“距离越远越不重要”的可控衰减。MoSAR 试图把这个问题改写成 attention geometry learning：让模型学习不同 token 对在不同语义条件下应采用何种距离衰减规律，从而在不硬切断远程依赖的前提下，形成可近似、可裁剪的软稀疏结构。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义不在于又提出一种稀疏 attention 变体，而在于给出了一种更接近训练动态的视角：高效注意力不一定要先定义稀疏图，再让模型适应；也可以先学习“哪些位置关系值得保留”的几何，再在推理时把已经充分衰减的区域近似掉。这个思路对长上下文预训练有两点启发。第一，位置编码与稀疏化不必分离，衰减几何可以作为 RoPE 之后的补充 inductive bias。第二，训练时保持软、连续、可微的几何，推理时再做离散近似，可能比从一开始就使用硬 mask 更稳。量级上，这篇工作更像中等规模、机制导向的架构探索，不是已经证明可直接替代主流 dense attention 的成熟 recipe，但它提出的“learned decay geometry”值得继续跟进。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 Mixture of Semantic Attention Regimes。它在 post-RoPE 的 Q/K 路径上加入轻量双线性路由器，而不是只路由 query 或只路由 key。原因很直接：只路由一侧只能决定 attention 矩阵的行或列结构，难以在二维平面上预先定位“哪些区域可能重要”；双侧路由后，query 位置 i 和 key 位置 j 的 regime 组合共同决定 pairwise interaction 属于哪一种语义衰减机制。\n\n每个 semantic regime 对应一个固定的距离门控函数 g(d)。这个函数不是简单指数衰减，而是带有三个区段：近距离 plateau 区域基本保留 RoPE 交互；中间 transition 区域平滑衰减；远距离 low-relevance 区域压到很低，从而暴露出可忽略的 attention 区域。这样做有两个目的：一是避免硬局部 mask 带来的语义断点；二是让“可近似性”从训练目标里自然长出来，而不是事后再做启发式裁剪。\n\n路由器是 input-conditioned 的，且作用在位置编码之后，因此 regime 选择同时依赖内容表示和带位置信息的 Q/K 几何。论文强调 semantic 一词，就是为了区分仅按相对距离或固定窗口决定局部性的做法。MoSAR-Cost 则在此基础上加入显式 cost pressure，偏置路由器选择更短 expected reach 的 regime，把困惑度与计算范围之间的权衡前移到训练中。\n\n和已有方法相比，差异主要有三点。第一，它不是预定义稀疏 pattern，而是预定义一组衰减 law，让 pattern 由数据驱动地涌现。第二，它不是直接删边，而是先连续衰减，再利用衰减后的低值区域做近似。第三，它把高效 attention 问题表述成几何建模问题，而不是单纯的算子替换问题。","experiments":"实验以 500M 参数、from-scratch 预训练为主，重点比较 dense RoPE、固定 locality 基线、MoSAR 以及带成本约束的 MoSAR-Cost。论文给出的主结论是：在作者评测的变体中，adaptive controlled decay 在 4096 和 8192 token 长度上都优于 dense RoPE 与固定局部注意力基线，MoSAR 的 perplexity 最好。摘要没有给出完整数值表，但从正文描述看，收益主要出现在长上下文区间，而不是短序列上的普遍提升，这与方法目标一致。\n\n更关键的是推理阶段的近似实验。作者把训练时的软 mixture routing 在推理时替换成 deterministic top-1 routing，用单一 regime 近似每个交互区域，结果是 expected normalized reach 明显下降，同时在长度外推下仍保持有竞争力的 perplexity。这个结果说明 regime mixture 学到的不是脆弱的训练技巧，而是确实形成了可离散化的几何结构。MoSAR-Cost 进一步把 trade-off 往更低 reach 推，代价是一定程度的语言建模损失；这部分实验的价值在于它把“效率-质量”关系显式参数化了，而不是只报一个点。\n\n如果精读正文，建议特别看消融：单侧路由 vs 双侧路由、不同 regime 数量、是否需要平滑 transition、cost regularization 强度、以及长度外推设置。这里最值得关注的不是绝对困惑度，而是 reach、近似误差和 PPL 三者之间是否呈现稳定单调关系。如果关系稳定，这类方法才有机会扩展到更大模型。","reading_guide":"这篇适合泛读偏精读之间的方式。先看 Introduction，抓住作者的问题重写：不是“怎么设计更好的稀疏模式”，而是“怎么学习 attention 的衰减几何”。然后直接看方法部分里 semantic regime 的定义、双线性 query/key routing 的公式，以及 MoSAR-Cost 的目标项，理解为什么一定要在 post-RoPE 做路由。\n\n实验部分优先看三类图表：一是不同上下文长度下的 perplexity 对比；二是 expected reach 或 normalized reach 的统计；三是 top-1 routing 与软 mixture 的近似结果。若时间有限，重点看能否回答三个问题：学习到的 regime 是否真的分化出不同局部性；这种分化是否随层和 token 语义变化；推理时离散化后损失是否可控。预计 35 到 50 分钟能抓住主线；若要判断是否值得复现，需要再看附录里的实现图、复杂度讨论和更多消融。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper tackles a specific bottleneck in long-context self-attention: deciding which query-key interactions should be preserved, softly down-weighted, or made negligible should not be hard-coded in advance. Most efficient attention methods impose a fixed sparsity pattern such as windows or block masks. That assumes a universal notion of locality across tokens, layers, and contexts. RoPE, on the other hand, gives a relative-position geometry but does not provide an explicit, controllable decay of relevance with distance. MoSAR reframes the problem as learning an attention geometry: the model should infer, from content and position together, which distance-decay regime applies to each interaction.","importance":"The contribution is less about introducing yet another sparse attention variant and more about changing the design lens. Instead of specifying a sparse graph first and asking the model to live with it, the paper learns a family of decay geometries and lets approximate sparsity emerge from training. For pretraining research, this matters in two ways. First, positional encoding and efficiency need not be separate concerns; a learned decay geometry can complement RoPE rather than replace it. Second, keeping the geometry soft and differentiable during training, then discretizing or approximating it at inference, may be a more stable path than training with hard masks from the start. This is still a medium-scale architectural study rather than a production-ready replacement for dense attention, but the learned-decay perspective is technically meaningful.","technical_approach":"$10","experiments":"$11","reading_guide":"$12","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"只看方法和效率曲线；若路由开销小且可训练稳定，值得放进长上下文 attention ablation。","en":"Read the method and efficiency curves; if routing overhead is small and training is stable, it deserves a long-context attention ablation."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Università degli Studi di Bari Aldo Moro","Affiliation:Department of Computer Science","Affiliation:Università degli Studi di Bari Aldo Moro","Affiliation:Bari"],"authors":["Michele Paolicelli","Alessandro Petruzzelli","Alessandro Franceso Maria Martina","Cataldo Musto","Giovanni Semeraro"],"notable_authors":[],"tags":["attention-architecture","long-context","adaptive-sparsity","routing","efficient-attention"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.31261","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2609.31261","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2609.30977","title":"Does Uniform Discrete Diffusion Need Time?","abstract":"Uniform discrete diffusion models (UDMs) commonly use explicit time conditioning, but we find that it can often be unnecessary in practice. In this paper, we first show that the population-optimal UDM predictor generally depends on time: time controls how much the model should trust the observed context. We then show that this dependence can become negligible in finite-data settings relevant to language. When a corrupted training sequence remains much closer to its original clean sequence than t","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-25T08:24:35Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2609_30977.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"核心问题是：Uniform Discrete Diffusion Model 是否真的需要显式 time conditioning。UDM 与 masked diffusion 不同，噪声 token 是普通词表 token，模型无法直接知道当前位置是否被污染；因此 diffusion time 理论上提供了“当前上下文可信度”的额外信息。论文要解释一个现象：population optimum 明明依赖时间，但大语言 UDM 里 time-agnostic predictor 仍然经常工作得很好。","importance":"这篇论文对 diffusion LM 的架构取舍有直接参考价值。它把“是否加时间条件”从经验问题拆成了两个层面：总体分布上的最优预测器通常需要时间；有限训练集上的经验最优预测器在大部分噪声区间可能几乎不需要时间。这个结论能解释 LLaDA/MDLM 风格的 time-agnostic 设计为什么在 masked setting 自然成立，也能解释 UDM 中为什么只有高噪声区间更依赖时间。对预训练研究的启发在于：某些条件变量在 population view 下有信息量，但在有限数据、稀疏离散空间和训练样本高度分离的 regime 下，其边际收益会明显收缩。","technical_approach":"技术路线分三步。第一，分析 UDM 的 population-optimal predictor。Uniform corruption 会把真实 token 和随机替换 token 混在同一个词表空间里，所以每个 observed token 都可能是可靠上下文，也可能是噪声。最优预测需要对“哪些位置可靠”的所有假设做加权；time 不改变这些假设本身，而是通过噪声强度改变假设的相对权重。这里的关键量是类似 rho_t = K alpha_t / (1 - alpha_t) 的信噪比，它决定观察到的 token 应该被信任到什么程度。第二，转向有限数据经验分布。语言序列空间极大，训练样本之间通常在 Hamming 距离上分离得很开。一个 corrupted sequence 在大部分噪声水平下仍然更接近它的原始 clean sequence，而不是其他训练样本。这样 posterior 会集中到少数候选上，time 对候选重加权的作用变小。第三，给出基于 post-corruption separation margin 的 time-sensitivity bound，并用语言数据验证：保证在高噪声端点附近变弱，因为这时多个 clean sequence/hypothesis 都可能解释同一个 corrupted sequence。","experiments":"实验重点不是做一个新 UDM，而是测 time 的实际使用程度。结果显示，训练后的语言 UDM 在 diffusion trajectory 的大部分区间对 time conditioning 不敏感；time sensitivity 在高噪声区域明显上升。显式 time conditioning 的预测收益也主要集中在高噪声阶段。一个只在高噪声 regime 打开 time conditioning 的 hybrid model，性能接近全程 time-conditioned model。完全 time-agnostic UDM 在多个数据集和训练目标下仍有竞争力，并且在若干设置中优于 time-conditioned 对照。论文还验证了语言训练数据在大多数噪声水平下存在较大的 post-corruption separation，这支撑了有限数据理论解释。","reading_guide":"建议泛读 30 到 45 分钟。优先看 Introduction 中 UDM 与 MDM 的差异，这决定了为什么 masked diffusion 的 time-agnostic 结论不能直接迁移到 UDM。理论部分重点看 population-optimal predictor 的分解：time 如何只通过可靠上下文假设的权重进入预测。然后看 finite-data bound，尤其是 separation margin 与 time sensitivity 的关系。实验部分建议看 time sensitivity 随噪声水平变化的图，以及 hybrid time conditioning 的对照结果。最值得深挖的是高噪声区间：如果只在这一段引入 time embedding，可能在保持架构简单的同时拿到大部分收益。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The central question is whether uniform discrete diffusion models need explicit time conditioning. Unlike masked diffusion, UDM corruption replaces tokens with ordinary vocabulary tokens, so the model cannot directly tell whether an observed token is clean or noise. Diffusion time can therefore encode how much the observed context should be trusted. The work explains why the population-optimal UDM predictor is time-dependent, while time-agnostic predictors can still work well in language-scale finite-data settings.","importance":"The result is useful for diffusion LM design. It separates two regimes that are often conflated: at the population level, time usually carries information; under finite empirical language data, its practical marginal value can be small across most of the trajectory. This helps explain why time-agnostic predictors are natural for masked diffusion and can remain competitive for UDMs, even though UDMs do not reveal corrupted positions. For pretraining, the broader lesson is that an input variable may be theoretically informative but weakly used when the empirical data distribution is sparse and training sequences are well separated.","technical_approach":"$13","experiments":"The empirical analysis measures how much trained language UDMs use time. The models show weak time sensitivity over most of the diffusion trajectory, with a clear increase in the high-noise region. The predictive gain from explicit time conditioning is similarly concentrated near high noise. A hybrid model that enables time conditioning only in that region performs close to a fully time-conditioned model. Fully time-agnostic UDMs remain competitive across datasets and training objectives, and in some settings outperform time-conditioned counterparts. Additional measurements show that language data maintain large post-corruption separation over most noise levels, matching the finite-data explanation.","reading_guide":"A 30–45 minute skim is enough for most readers. The finite-data margin bound is the main theoretical object to inspect. In the experiments, prioritize the time-sensitivity curves and the hybrid-conditioning ablation. both","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-09-25T02:31:02Z","category_id":"architecture","cover_url":"/covers/2609_30706.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.AI","cs.CL"],"summary":{"core_problem":"LAVOIR 解决的是单次前向决策 encoder 在信息不完整时不会追问的问题：模型不仅要给出 typed decision 的概率，还要判断问哪个缺失 slot 最可能提升正确决策概率。","importance":"这篇论文对基座预训练的直接影响较弱，更接近决策模型和后训练数据构造。不过它有一个有用启发：可以把 value of information 做成监督信号，让非生成式 encoder 在一次前向中同时输出决策分布和澄清问题价值。这对工具路由、客服分流、低延迟分类系统有参考意义，也对“什么时候该问而不是猜”的训练目标设计有借鉴。它的价值不在大模型规模，而在把可枚举 schema、规则 gold、模拟回答和校准概率结合起来，形成不依赖人工标注的 VOI 训练信号。","technical_approach":"LAVOIR 扩展了 Laya/Jev 风格的 System One 决策接口。输入中原本有问题类型、指令、每个选项对应的 [MASK] marker 和状态文本；LAVOIR 在选项之后追加一个 missing information slot block，每个候选缺失信息也有一个 marker。模型保留 Laya 在 ModernBERT-large 上的决策 head，并新增三个组件：零初始化的 segment embedding，使没有 slot block 隐层和标量输出。训练目标不需要人工标注：schema 规则把完整 profile 映射到 gold unit，LLM 只负责把 profile verbalize 成自然语言消息和答案；对同一消息配多个隐藏 slot 值，通过询问后 p_k(y*) 与询问前 p_0(y*) 的差估计 realized gain，并用最小二乘回归学习 amortized VOI。决策策略是：若最大预测 VOI recipe 中的 policy-gradient 项对该设置没有带来收益，并且会拖慢 VOI head 的学习；按 question type 使用单一温度会在异质混合问题上造成校准偏差。可确认的模型规模细节包括底座为 ModernBERT-large，新增 VOI head 约 0.27M 参数。","reading_guide":"建议泛读，预计 20 到 30 分钟。优先看 Problem
